                                PATIRRUPPATTU
                               1. Introduction
                   By Prof. T. P. Meenakshisundaran, Avl.

       Tiru Avvai Duraiswami Pillai of the Annamalai University is well
       known as the modern commentator on Sangam works. He now gives the
       world of Scholars his commentary on Patirruppattu.
       Patirrupattu is the only available source book of ancient Cera
       history. Among all the Sangam works this is unique in being a
       collection of the poems on Ceras. Ten great poets had sung the
       greatness of ten great Cera kings. Unfortunately of the hundred
       verses, the first ten and the last ten, probably singing the
       glories of Utiyan and Yanaikkan Mantaram Ceral Irumporai
       respectively, are missing. There were two ruling Cera families that
       of Utiyan and that of Antuvan, the former being the senior and the
       main Cera line of Vanchi: the latter being the Irumpoprai line of
       Tondi. The second Ten and the third Ten belong to the two sons of
       Uthiyan viz. Imaya Varampan Netun Ceral Athan and
       Palyanaic-celkezhu Kuttuvan. The foruth, the fifth and the sixth
       Tens relate respectively to the sons of Netun Ceral Athan viz.
       Kalamkay-k-kanni Nar Muti-c-ceral, Katal Pirakkottiya Cemkuttuvan,
       and Atukot-Pattu-c-ceral Athan. The rest of the book relate to the
       Irumporai family: the VII Ten belongs to Selvakkatunko Vazhi Athan,
       the VIII Ten to Perum Ceral Irumporai the conqueror of Thakatur and
       the IX Ten to Ilam Ceral Irumporai, the son, the grandson and the
       great grandson respectively of Antuvan Ceral.
       Various places of importance, the great chiefs and chieftains, the
       foreigners like Yavanas, the warfare and important battle field,
       the fivefold classification of the country and other geographical
       details are all alluded to in this anthology. The poets of this
       anthology and the literary patronage of the Ceras are not myths.
       Paranan Kannam Kannan Katu, Vezhakkadu (Umbarkadu) and Kakkaiyur,
       still existing in Malayalam country, bear testimony to the
       conferment of Jaghirs on the poets Paranar, Kumattoor Kannanar and
       Kakkai-p-Padiniyar by the Chera kings, Cenkuttuvan, Imayavaramban
       Netumceralathan, and Atukotpattu-c-Ceral Athan repectively.
       This book is important as revealing the culture, the custom and
       practice, the political and military life of the Tamil land, of the
       first few centuries of the Christian era. It is interesting to note
       that the conception of the fundamental unity of India, from the
       Himalayas to Cape Comorin, goes back, to the poems of the Sangam
       Age (Verse1 and 43 of this anthology). Politics had become, by that
       time, an all India science and the Yagas, varying according to the
       graduation of sovereignity, were believed to have a magical
       influence in establishing an ever expanding empire. This inspired
       the ambitious kings of Tamil land to perform the Royal sacrifices;
       and the study of their scred texts and related literature became
       very popular. Further, the political unrest in the North drove the
       Sanskrit, Pali and Prakrit scholars to South India. Manimekalai,
       the Tamil epic reveals the Tamil land as a centre of all- India
       learning, with its intellectual capital at Kancipuram. The
       beginning of this development is seen in Patirrupattu itself.
       The Yagas are mentioned in many places. The Rajasuya was not
       unknown; there was a Cola of the name Rajasuya Vetta Peru Nar'
       Killi ie, Nal Killi the great who had performed the Rajasuya Yaga
       There was the great Pandya of many Yagas viz. Palyakacalai Muthu
       Kutimi-p-Peru Vazhuthi. The Ceras, as seen from this book, were no
       exceptions. The Putra Kameshti yaga is, it may be suggested, men-
       tioned in poem No. 74 by Arisilkizhar, a Non-Brahmin poet. There we
       learn that the queen donned on her beautiful shoulders a circular
       skin of the stag, well cut out and decked with pearls and precious
       stones. Was the skin made thus into a valuable amulet? In the
       Atharva Veda are two charms for obtaining a son ( III, 23 & VI.11).
       The second of these mantras is relevant to the study of this
       Putrakameshti. This consists of three parts and when the third
       part,which runs thus "May Prajapati elsewhere afford the birth of a
       female but here shall bestow a man" was recited, acoording to the
       sutras (Kans.35-9) which explain the procedure, "The fire is
       surrounded with the wool of a male animal and the wool is tied upon
       the woman" (probably as an amulet). Instead of the wool we find the
       skin of the stag, the male animal, being used in the sacrifice
       performed by the Cera king.
       This reference to this Putra-kameshti is very important. The
       question is very often raised whether the Ceras who ruled over a
       country where the Marumakkal Tayam or matriarchal succession now
       reigns supreme, were following the law of Makkal Tayam or
       patriarchial succession, as their colleagues the Colas anmd the
       Pandyas did, or were following the law of Marumakkal Tayam, as
       followed by the kings of Malayalam today. The wife of the king
       under the Marumakkal Tayam is never called the Queen. In
       Pattirrupattu, every Cera king is praised as the husband of the
       chaste consort,. But this does not help us to decide the issue
       raised. The Putra kameshti poem, however, conclusively pro- ves, it
       was the son of the consort that becomes the king, thus establish-
       ing beyond doubt the patriarchal succession of these kings. There
       is one other difficulty, for the Padikam speaks of the mothers of
       these kings as " Velavikoman Pathuman Devi " etc. It is this kind
       of expression that has really created the confusion. Devi
       ordinarily means a wife; this phrase will then denote a wife of a
       Velir chief Pathuman etc. How can the wife of a chief be also wife
       of the Cera king? This lead some scholars to assume that the
       succession was matriarchal, where the mother of the ruling prince
       is never the wife of the king. But as this theory runs counter to
       the explicit statement of the Putra kameshti poem, one has to
       explain the word according to Tiru T.V Sadasiva Panda- rathar as
       meaning "daughter", a usage made clear by such phrases as Ceran ma
       Devi, Pancavan ma Devi, all wives of Colas and not of Ceras or
       Pandyas. Or Ceran ma devi etc. may be an elliptical short hand
       expression for "The great Cola queen the daughter of the Pandya or
       Cera". Nam,es like Sentan korran or Kiran Korran etc. mean Korran
       son of Centan or Kiran . Therefore Patuman Madevi may mean the
       Queen, the daughter of Pathuman. One is justified in referring to
       the usage of the Imperial Cola inscriptions in view of the close
       kinship that exists between the Meykkirtis of the later Colas and
       the Patikams of Patirrupattu, as explained by tiru T.V. Sadasiva
       Pandaratar in his short introduction to the present commentary.
       In other places also, the Cera kings are praised in Pattirupattu,
       for bringing forth children. The theory of three debts or duties to
       the Devas, the ancestors, and the learned, to be discharged respec-
       tively, by every man, by performing sacrifices, by bringing forth
       child- ren, and by learning the ancient texts, is, as seen by this
       very poem and poem No. 70 as old as the Sangam age. Therefore, when
       Parimel- azahgar speaks of this in his commentary on the chapter on
       children in Tirukkural, he is not going against any Tamil
       tradition. There is an old commentary on Patirruppattu, whose
       importance cannot be easily exaggerated; but to the modern reader
       it is but a series of algebraical formula. The present commentary
       explains all that is necessary for elucidating the text for the man
       in the street-the defied abstraction of modern democracy; and
       serves, therefore, as a valuable key for unlocking the closely
       preserved treasure-chest of the old commentary.
       The Sangam poets caught the spirit of the poetic moment and
       enshrined it in the most appropriate and correct poetic phrase,
       afraid of spoiling its effect by any elaboration. Brevity is the
       life of their poetry. This explains why the anthologies of the
       Sangam verses are arranged according to the length of verses, as
       Kurum Tokai, Nedum Tokai, Aim Kuru Nuru etc. In every poem there is
       the heart or life centre-a beautiful phrase which is the very quint
       essence of the poem and this phrase by common acceptance and
       appreciation, becomes according to Sangam usage, the very name of
       the verse. This is seen in every one of the verses in the present
       work. The new commentary has brought out the poetic significance of
       all these poetic phrases, thus helping us to understand the poetic
       theory of the Sangam age.
       On an occasion like this, one cannot but think with reverential
       gratitude, of the late lamented Dr.Swaminatha Aiyar, but of whose
       untiring zeal and toil we could not be boasting either of
       Patirruppattu or its ancient commentary. What has been thus made
       available is now made more and more popular by the present
       commentator. My friend Tiru Vellaivaranan has brought out the
       importance of the new commentary. Whether one agrees with all the
       points or not, one has to agree that this commentary is very
       valuable for a close study of the ancient work by the modern man. I
       deem it, therefore a great privilege, as desired by my friend Tiru
       Avvai Duraiswami Pillai, to introduce, with heartfelt thanks, this
       work, to the over grateful public of Tamil land.
       Madras 26-10-49 T.P. Meenakshisundaram.
       -------

                                 patippurai

       centamiḻnāṭṭuk karuvūlamākat tikaḻvaṉa eṭṭuttokai nūlkaḷum
       piṟavumām. eṭṭuttokaiyiṉ vaippumuṟai oru ciṟanta kuṟikkōḷuṭaṉ
       amaikkappeṟṟateṉa ataṉ kāpput tiruppāṭṭai vaittu orupuṭai yāka
       vuṇaralām. atu, "māyōṉ mēya kāṭuṟai yulakamum" eṉṉum
       tolkāppiyattaip piṉpaṟṟiyatākum.
       avaṟṟuḷ 'patiṟṟuppattu' nāṉkām eṇmuṟaikkaṇ amaiyap peṟṟatu. mutaṉ
       mūṉṟum akapporuḷaiyum itu puṟapporuḷaiyum uṇarttuvaṉa. ivaṟṟiṉ
       kuṟippu akapporuḷ kaikkiḷai, aintiṇai peruntiṇai eṉum
       pākupāṭeṉalām.
       patiṟṟuppattu puṟapporuḷai yuṇarttuvatumaṭṭumaṉṟip paṇ ṭaic
       cēramaṉṉar varalāṟṟuk kuṟippukkaḷait tokuttuṇarap perum pāṉmaiyum
       tuṇaiceyyum ciṟanta karuvinūlākum. innūṟkup paḻaiya uraiyoṉṟuḷḷatu.
       atu, pulavarkaḷukkumaṭṭum viḷaṅkum taṉmaiyatu.
       innūṟkuc coṟporuḷ, karuttu, viḷakkam, ilakkaṇak kuṟippu, oppu
       mutaliya iṉṟiyamaiyā vuṟuppukkaḷ palavamainta akalavurai yoṉṟu
       illātiruntamai peruṅkuṟai. akkuṟai akaṉṟu kaṟpārkku eḻucciyum
       iṉpamum tarumāṟu ōr urai iyaṟṟittara, aṇṇāmalaip palkalaik kaḻakat
       tamiḻ ārāycci virivuraiyāḷar cittānta kalāniti, vittuvāṉ tiru.
       auvai cu. turaicāmip piḷḷaiyavarkaḷaik kēṭṭuk koṇṭōm. avarkaḷum
       eṅkaḷ vēṇṭukōḷukkiṇaṅki, av vaṇṇamē iyaṟṟit tantaṉar.
       ivarkaḷ caṅkat taṇṭamiḻ nūlkaḷil tōyntu uṇmaip poruḷ kaṇṭa
       oṇmaiyar. 'puṟanāṉūṟu' mutaliyavaṟṟiṟkuc ciṟanta urai
       iyaṟṟiyuḷḷavarkaḷ. ivvurai eḷimai iṉimai tiṭpanuṭpam teḷivu
       viḷakkam mutaliya uraip paṇpukaḷ ciṟantuḷḷatu. uraiyāciriyar
       avarkaṭku eṅkaḷ naṉṟi urittākuka.
       nūlaippaṟṟiyum uraiyāciriyar urainayam mutaliyavaṟṟaip paṟṟiyum
       takkavāṟāyntu paṉmoḻippulavar, tiru te. po. mīṉāṭcicuntaram
       piḷḷaiyavarkaḷ ōr aṇinturai āṅkilattil utaviyuḷḷārkaḷ. iv
       vurainalattaip pārāṭṭi aṇṇāmalaip palkalaik kaḻakat tamiḻārāycit
       tuṟai virivuraiyāḷar, tiru. ka. veḷḷaivāraṇaṉār avarkaḷum
       patiṟṟuppattiṉ patikaṅkaḷiṉ karuttu viḷaṅka aṇṇāmalaip palkalaik
       kaḻaka ārāycci virivuraiyāḷar tiru. ti. vi. catācivap
       paṇṭārattāravar kaḷum ciṟanta āyvuraikaḷ eḻuti utaviyuḷḷārkaḷ.
       immūvarukkum eṅkaḷ naṉṟi urittākuka.
       innūlaip paḻantamiḻuṇmaikaṇṭuvakkum perumakkaḷum māṇāk karum
       āciriyarkaḷum vāṅkip payaṉpeṟuvārkaḷāka.
       caivacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakattār.

                    patiṟṟuppattu mūlamum - uraiyum
                               2. muṉṉurai

   kaṟṟataṉā lāya payaṉeṉkol vālaṟivaṉ
       naṟṟāḷ toḻāar eṉiṉ.

   caṅkat tokai nūlkaḷ eṭṭut tokaiyum pattup pāṭṭumeṉa vakaiyāl
   iraṇṭākiyum viriyāl patiṉeṭṭākiyum nilavuvaṉavākum. ivaṟṟuḷ
   patiṟṟuppatteṉpatu eṭṭut tokaiyuḷ oṉṟākum. itu pappattāka amainta
   pattukkaḷ pattukoṇṭateṉṟum, nūṟu pāṭṭukkaḷ koṇṭateṉṟum patiṟṟuppattu
   eṉappaṭum peyarālē aṟiyalām. itaṉ ovvoru pattum ovvoru cēravēntaṉaip
   paṟṟi vēṟu vēṟu āciriyaṉmārkaḷāl pāṭappaṭṭuḷḷatu. inta āciriyaṉmārkaḷum
   cēravēntarkaḷum orukālattavarākavum vēṟu vēṟu kālattavarākavum
   karutappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. patiṟṟuppatteṉṉum peyar ippāṭṭukkaḷaip pappattāka
   eṭuttut tokuttōrāl iṭappeṟṟa peyarātal vēṇṭum.
   eṭṭut tokaiyuḷ kāṇappaṭum naṟṟiṇai, kuṟuntokai, aiṅkuṟunūṟu,
   patiṟṟuppattu, paripāṭal, kalittokai, akanāṉūṟu, puṟanāṉūṟu eṉṟa
   eṭṭaṉuḷ, puṟapporuḷ vakaiyil amaintavai patiṟṟuppattum, puṟanāṉūṟum
   eṉṟa iraṇṭumākum. ivaiyiraṇṭum, ēṉaiyavaṟṟuḷ naṟṟiṇai kalittokaiyeṉṟa
   iraṇṭu moḻinta piṟa yāvum kālañceṉṟa perum pulavar ṭākṭar . tiru.u.vē.
   cāminātaiyaravarkaḷāl pala āṇṭukaṭku muṉpē pala ēṭukaḷiṉ tuṇaikoṇṭu
   ārāyntu cevviya muṟaiyil veḷiyiṭappeṟṟuḷḷaṉa. avaṟṟuḷ patiṟṟuppattu
   1904-ām āṇṭil mutaṉmutalāka acciṭappeṟṟu veḷiyāyiṟṟu. ivvāṟu
   tamiḻakattut tamiḻaratu ilakkiyap paḻañ celvamāy, pala nūṟṟāṇṭukaṭku
   muṉ vāḻnta tamiḻ naṉmakkaḷatu nākarikap paṇpāṭukaḷai yuṇarttum
   nalviḷakkamāyt tikaḻum intat tokai nūlkaḷaic cemmaiyuṟa ārāyntu iṉṟum
   eṉṟum tamiḻ payilvōr aṉaivarum orumukamākap pāraṭṭip paravattakka
   vakaiyil veḷiyiṭuvatākiya ariya tamiḻppaṇi purinta
   ṭākṭar.tiru.ayyaravarkaṭkut tamiḻulaku peritum kaṭamaippaṭṭuḷatu.
   tiru. ayyaravarkaḷ mutaṉmutalāka veḷiyiṭṭapōtē itaṟkup paṇṭaic cāṉṟōr
   oruvar eḻutiya uraiyum avarkku uṭaṉ kiṭaittamaiyiṉ, mutaṉ veḷiyīṭṭilē
   patiṟṟuppattu mūlamum paḻaiya uraiyum eṉa veḷiyiṭṭu, itaṉai aṟiñar
   kaṇṭu poruḷaṟintu iṉpuṟum vāyilaiyum uta viyatu peritum
   pōṟṟattakkatākum. "ivvurai illaiyāyiṉ, innūṟporuḷai ikkālattil
   aṟintukoḷḷutal mika aritu" eṉa avarkaḷē uraittiruppatu kāṇka. ēṭukaḷil
   uraikārar peyar kuṟikkappaṭāmaiyiṉ avar iṉṉāreṉṟu aṟivatu iyalātāyiṟṟu.
   āyiṉum avar nēminātam ceyta kuṇavīra paṇṭitar kālattukkup piṟpaṭṭava
   reṉpatu orutalai. patiṟ ṟuppattiṉ uraiyil, cillērāḷa reṉṟaviṭattuc
   "ciṉmai yaic ciṉṉūl eṉṟatupōla īṇṭuc ciṟumaiyākak koḷka" (patiṟ. 76;
   11) eṉṟaviṭattu uraikārarāl ciṉṉūl eṉap paṭuvatu kuṇavīra paṇṭitar
   eḻutiya nēmināta meṉṉum ilakkaṇa nūlukkup peyar.
   ivvaṇṇam pāṭṭum uraiyumākak kiṭaikkum ippatiṟ ṟuppattu, mutaṟ pattum
   pattām pattum iṉṟi iṭainiṉṟa eṭṭup pattukkaḷē koṇṭuḷḷatu. kiṭaikkum
   ēṭukaḷ palavaṟṟiṉum mutaṟ pattum iṟutippattum kāṇappaṭavillai. pāṭṭum
   uraiyum koṇṭa ēṭoṉṟil, oṉpatām pattiṉ iṟutiyil, "patiṟṟuppattu mūlamum
   uraiyum muṭintatu; cupa mastu" eṉṟu kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. mūlamēyuḷḷa
   ēṭukaḷum oṉ patām pattōṭē muṭikiṉṟaṉa. ivvāṟē toṭakkamum iraṇṭām
   pattaiyē koṇṭu pala ēṭukaḷum uḷḷaṉa. itaṉāl neṭunāṭkaḷukkumuṉpē, itaṉ
   mutaṟ pattum pattām pattum maṟaintaṉa eṉak koḷḷavēṇṭiyirukkiṟatu.
   iṉi, kiṭaittuḷḷa ovvoru pattukkum patikam oṉṟu kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. atu,
   pāṭappaṭṭa cēravēntaṉuṭaiya meyk kīrttipōl niṉṟu pāṭiṉōr, pāṭappaṭṭōr,
   pāṭiya pāṭṭu, pāṭippeṟṟa paricil, pāṭappaṭṭa vēntaṉuṭaiya āṭcik kālam
   eṉṟa ivaṟṟaik kūṟukiṟatu. appatikaṅkaḷ, tām uṭ koṇṭuniṟkum pāṭṭukkaḷ
   ovvoṉṟukkum avvap pāṭṭi ṉuṭ kāṇappaṭum coṟṟoṭa roṉrāṟ peyar
   kuṟittuḷḷaṉa. ovvoru pāṭṭiṉ iṟutiyilum, appāṭṭiṟkut tuṟai, vaṇ ṇam,
   tūkku, peyar eṉṟa yivai vakukkap peṟṟuḷḷaṉa. ippeyarkaḷē patikattilum
   tokuttuk kāṭṭappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. mēlum ippatikaṅkaḷ uraiyuḷḷa ēṭukaḷiṟṟāṉ
   kāṇappaṭu kiṉṟaṉa. paḻaiyavurai ippatikaṅkaṭkum urai kuṟittuḷḷatu.
   āṉāl, ippatikaṅkaḷ uraiyillāta mūlappakutiyē yuḷḷa ēṭukaḷil illai.
   mūlamēyuḷḷa ēṭukaḷil ovvoru pāṭṭiṉ iṟutiyilum tuṟai, vaṇṇam, tūkku,
   peyar mutaliyaṉa kuṟikkap peṟṟum, ovvoru pattiyiṉ iṟutiyilum pāṭiṉōr
   peyarum pāṭappaṭṭōr peyarum, “imaya varampaṉ neṭuñ cēralātaṉaik
   kumaṭṭūrk kaṇṇaṉār pāṭiṉār pattuppāṭṭu muṟṟum” eṉṟāṟpōlak kuṟikkap
   peṟṟum uḷḷaṉa. ik kuṟippukaḷai nōkkumiṭattu, patiṟṟuppattup pāṭṭukaḷait
   tokuttōrāl vaṇṇam, tūkku, tuṟai, peyar mutaliyaṉa vakukkap
   peṟṟirukkalāmeṉṟum, avarkkuppiṉ vanta cāṉ ṟōr oruvarāl patikaṅkaḷ pāṭic
   cērkkappeṟṟirukkalā meṉṟum karututaṟku iṭamuṇṭākiṟatu. ṭākṭar tiru ayya
   ravarkaḷum, “ovvoru ceyyuḷiṉ piṉṉum amaintuḷḷa tuṟai, vaṇṇam, tūkku,
   peyareṉpavaikaḷ uraiyillāta mūlap piratikaḷilellām iruttaliṉ, avai
   uraiyāciriyarāl eḻutappaṭṭaṉa valla veṉṟum patikaṅkaḷ uraip pirati
   kaḷil maṭṭum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟamaiyāl avaṟṟai iyaṟṟiṉōr nūlāciriya
   rallareṉṟum terikiṉṟaṉa. āciriyar nacciṉārk kiṉiyarālum aṭiyārkku
   nallārālum tattam uraikaḷil eṭut tāḷap peṟṟiruttaliṉ, ippatikaṅkaḷ
   avarkaḷ kālattiṟku muntiyavai yeṉṟu tōṟṟukiṟaṉtu” eṉṟu kūṟuvatu nōk
   kattakkatu.
   cumār patiṉaintu āṇṭukaṭkumuṉ ceṉṉaip palkalaik kaḻaka vittuvāṉ
   tērvukkuc celvōr cilarukku ippatiṟ ṟuppattiṉaik kaṟpikka vēṇṭiya
   kaṭamai eṉakku uṇṭā yiṟṟu. akkālattē avarkaṭku itaṉaik kaṟpittu
   varukaiyil, itaṟkoru virivurai yiruppiṉ nalamākumeṉum eṇṇamuṇ ṭāka, eṉ
   naṇpar cilar vāyilākap patiṟṟuppattiṉ ēṭukaḷ iraṇṭu peṟṟu
   accuppaṭiyaiyum avaṟṟaiyum oppu nōkku vatu mutaṟkaṇ ceyaṟpālateṉa
   uṇarntu ceytatil cila pāṭa vēṟupāṭukaḷum, cila pāṭaṅkaḷiṟ kāṉappaṭṭa
   aiyaṅkaṭkut teḷivum kiṭaittaṉa. piṉṉar ivvuraippaṇiyai mēṟ koṇṭu ceytu
   varuṅkāl, tiruvaiyāṟṟu aracar kallūrip pērāciriyarum eṉ
   vaṇakkattiṟkuriya āciriyarumākiya karantaik kaviyaracu tiru. R.
   vēṅkaṭācalam piḷḷai yavarkaḷ tām eḻuti vaittirunta uraiyiṉaiyum tantu
   utaviṉārkaḷ. yāṉ eḻuti vaittirunta uraiyai nāvalar tiru.
   na.mu.vēṅkaṭacāmi nāṭṭāravarkaḷ pārvaiyiṭṭu ippōtu veḷiyāki yirukkum
   inta muṟaiyaik kāṭṭi ivvakaiyil ivvuraiyai yamaikkumāṟu terivittārkaḷ.
   ivvuraiyiṉ amaitiyai nēriṟ kaṇṭu ūkkiyatōṭu viraiya acciṭuvatu
   nalameṉṟu teruṭṭiya eṅkaḷ arumpeṟal āciriyar tiru. na. mu.vēṅkaṭacāmi
   nāṭṭāravarkaḷ, itaṉ pati kaṅkaḷ ārāyccikku iṭamāka vuḷḷaṉavātalāl,
   avaṟṟiṟku urai vēṇṭāveṉap paṇittamaiyiṉ, innūṟkaṇ patikaṅ kaṭku urai
   yeḻutappaṭavillai. mēlum, itaṉ mutaṟ pattu kiṭaikkāmaiyiṉ, itaṟku
   muṉṉaṇiyil kaṭavuḷ vāḻttuc ceyyuḷ oṉṟu iruntirukku meṉṟum,
   "eriyeḷḷuvaṉṉa niṟattaṉ" eṉat toṭaṅkuvatum, tolkāppiyap poruḷati kāra
   vuraiyil uraikārarāṟ kāṭṭappaṭuvatumākiya pāṭṭai ippatikattiṉ kaṭavuḷ
   vāḻttuc ceyyuḷākak kōttu urai yeḻutumāṟum avarkaḷ uraittārkaḷ. urai
   muḻutum accāki veḷivarum innāḷil avarkaḷ illāmai eṉ maṉattai
   varuttukiṟatu.
   iṉi, innūliṉ patikaṅkaḷukku urai yeḻutappaṭāmai yāl, ivaṟṟiṉ karuttu
   viḷaṅka oru kōvaippaṭutti varalāṟṟu muṟaiyil oru kaṭṭurai yeḻutic
   cērppatu nalameṉa ivvurai yaik kaṇṭa aṟiñar kūṟiṉar. iv varalāṟṟut
   tuṟaiyil yāṉ aṟinta nāṇmutal uḻaittup perumpayaṉ tamiḻulakiṟ kaḷittu
   varupavar eṉ arumpeṟal naṇpar tiru. T. V. catācivap paṇṭārattā
   ravarkaḷātalāl, avarkaḷai itaṉaic ceyyumāṟu vēṇṭikkoṇṭēṉ. avarkaḷum
   tamakkuḷḷa aluvalkaṭ kiṭaiyē uṭal valiyiṉmaiyum poruṭ paṭuttātu patikap
   pakutipaṟṟiya ciṟanta ārāycci yuraiyiṉai vaḻaṅki yuḷḷaṉar. avarkaṭku eṉ
   maṉaṅkeḻumiya naṉṟi yuriyatākiṉṟatu.
   iṉi, yāṉ eḻutiya uraiyai yāṉē ārāyntu nalan tīṅku kāṇpateṉpatu ciṟitu
   arumaiyāka iruntamaiyiṉ, urai kaṇṭa eṉṉaippōlavē pala āṇṭukaḷ vittuvāṉ
   tērvukkuc celvōrkku itaṉaik kaṟpikkum paṇi mēṟkoṇṭu kūrtta pulamaiyāl
   ciṟantu niṟkum eṉ ariya naṇpar tiru. ka. veḷḷai vāraṇa ravarkaḷai urai
   paṟṟiyatoru kaṭṭurai tarumāṟu kēṭ ṭukkoṇṭēṉ. ataṉai maṟātu ēṟṟu aḻakiya
   kaṭṭurai tanta avarkaṭkum eṉ māṉamārnta naṉṟi yuriyatākiṉṟatu.
   pallavar, cōḻar, pāṇṭiyar eṉṟa iv vēntarkaḷaip paṟṟiya varalāṟukaḷēṉum
   ōraḷavil makkaḷ aṟintu kōṭaṟ kēṟṟa vakaiyil nūlvaṭivil veḷiyāki
   irukkiṉṟaṉa; paṇṭaināḷaic cēra maṉṉarkaḷaippaṟṟiya varalāṟṟiṉaic cevvē
   aṟintu kōṭaṟku vēṇṭum karuvikaḷākac caṅkat tokai nūlkaṭku vēṟāka yātum
   kiṭaittilatu. iṭaik kālak kal veṭṭukkaḷ paṇṭaic caṅka kālattuc cērar
   varalāṟ ṟukkut tuṇai ceyvaṉavāka illai. cēranāṭṭu moḻiyum naṭaiyum
   oḻukalāṟum palavēṟu makkaḷ kūṭṭuṟavāl vēṟupaṭ ṭoḻintamai itaṟkuk
   kāraṇamām; āyiṉum, iṉṟaiya kēraḷa nāṭṭiṉ paṇṭaiya varalāṟṟiṉai
   ārāyntaṟitaṟku vēṇṭum karuvikaḷum kiṭaittila; kiṭaikkac ceytārumillai.
   ataṉāl ivvuraikkaṇ teḷivikkappaṭum varalāṟṟuk kuṟippuk kaḷ muṭinta
   muṭipākak kōṭaṟku iṭamillai yeṉpatu teḷi vām. vēṟu ārāyccikaḷāl
   viḷakkamākuṅkāṟum, ivaṟṟai mēṟkōṭal takkatu. immuyaṟcikkut tuṇaiceyta
   naṇparum, 1941-l vaṭavārkkāṭṭil jillāk kalvit talaivarāka iruntavaru
   māṉa tiru. irāmaṉ mēṉaṉ avarkaṭku eṉ naṉṟi yuriyatākum.
   ivvurai yeḻuti muṭikkappeṟṟatu cila pala āṇṭukaṭku muṉpēyeṉiṉum,
   iraṇṭāvatu ulakappōr kāraṇamāka, accēṟṟi veḷiyiṭuvateṉpatu ariya
   ceyalāyiṟṟu. an nerukkaṭi ippōtum tīrntu paṇṭupōl oḻintilatāyiṉum,
   ittakaiya cīriya centamiḻnūl veḷiyīṭukaḷil caliyātu uḻaittut
   tamiḻakattukkum tamiḻ moḻikkum peruntoṇṭu purintu ciṟakkum teṉṉintiya
   caiva cittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakam. ivvuraiyiṉai veḷiyiṭac camaintatu
   kuṟittup potu vāka, ataṟkum, ciṟappāka ataṉaip palavakai uyar paṇikaḷil
   īṭupaṭutti avvāṟṟāl aḵtu ulavāp perumpukaḻ uṇmait tamiḻ nilaiyamākat
   tikaḻacceytu mēmpaṭum kaḻakac ceyal muṟait talaivar, tiru. va.
   cuppaiyāppiḷḷai yavarkaṭkum tami ḻakattiṉ cārpil eṉ uḷam niṟainteḻum
   naṉṟiyiṉait terivittuk koḷḷukiṉṟēṉ.
   iṅkē kuṟitta tiruvāḷarkaḷē yaṉṟi, vēṟu vakaiyil tuṇaiceyta palarkkum eṉ
   naṉṟiyaic celuttum kaṭamai yuṭaiyēṉ.
   palvakaiyilum kuṟaipāṭuṭaiyaṉākiya eṉṉai, eṉ aru mait tamiḻaṉṉaikkut
   toṇṭaṉākki āṭkoṇṭaruḷum entai, tillai maṉṟil niṉṟu tiruvaruṭ
   kūttiyaṟṟum āṭalaracaṉ tiruvaṭip pōtukaḷai maṉamoḻi meykaḷāṟ paravi
   yamaikiṉṟēṉ.
   aṇṇāmalai nakar
   31-3-1949         auvai. cu. turaicāmi.

           3.patiṟṟuppattu uraiyil eṭuttāṇṭa nūlkaḷ

   tolkāppiyam collatikāram (tol. col.)
       tolkāppiyam collatikāram vēṟṟumai mayaṅkiyal (tol.col. vēṟ.mayaṅ.)
       tolkāppiyam collatikāram iṭaiyiyal (tol.col. iṭai)
       tolkāppiyam collatikāram eccaviyal(tol. col. ecca)

       tolkāppiyam puṟattiṇaiyiyal (tol. puṟat.)
       tolkāppiyam kaḷaviyal (tol. kaḷa.)
       tolkāppiyam kaṟpiyal (tol. kaṟ.)
       tolkāppiyam meyppāṭṭiyal (tol. mey.)
       tolkāppiyam uvamaviyal (tol.uvama.)
       tolkāppiyam ceyyuḷiyal (tol. cey.)
       tolkāppiyam marapiyal(tol. marapu.)
       naṟṟiṇai (naṟ.)
       kuṟuntokai (kuṟun.)

       patiṟṟuppattu (patiṟ.)

       puṟanāṉūṟu (puṟam.)
       tirumurukāṟṟuppaṭai (muruku.)
       porunarāṟṟuppaṭai (poruna.)
       ciṟu pāṇāṟṟuppaṭai (ciṟupāṇ.)
       perum pāṇāṟṟuppaṭai (perum pāṇ.)
       neṭunal vāṭai (neṭunal.)
       paṭṭiṉappālai (paṭṭi.)
